Um, how much time? God, it feels like all the time. Like, hours. I actually missed part of a big planning deadline recently because I spent so much time just... staring at how I looked in the draft event materials. Um, avoiding things? I mean... maybe? I don't know if I fully avoid them, but I definitely dread them. Like, I'll still go, but I'll try to stand in the back or volunteer to run the registration table so I don't have to mingle. 

Actually, speaking of registration tables, we used this new app for check-ins last week and it was a total disaster. The developer promised it would sync offline, but it totally didn't. The screens get so smudgy, and people always press way too hard on them.

I mean, yeah, definitely more than normal people. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I mean, I think it's incredibly obvious. Like, that's actually why I came in to see you in the first place. Ever since I got promoted and I have to be more in front of people at work, I just... I can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in photos. It's all I see. But when I point it out to my friends or my coworkers, they always say they barely see anything. Which is just crazy to me, because it's right there. 

But people are so unobservant anyway, you know? Like, my roommate, she literally didn't notice that our apartment building repainted the entire lobby. It used to be this really awful, depressing beige color, and they painted it this light sage green over the weekend. It smells like fresh paint everywhere. And she walked in on Monday and was like, "Did they change the lightbulbs in here?" Like, how do you not notice a completely different color on the walls? 

She's always been kind of oblivious, though. We went to high school together, and she used to date this guy who was so obviously cheating on her with a girl from the cheerleading squad. Everyone knew. He would literally leave his phone unlocked on the cafeteria table and you could see the texts popping up. But she just thought he was "really popular." I tried to tell her once, but she got so mad at me, so I just dropped it. We didn't talk for like three weeks, which was super awkward because we shared a locker. Having to do that little combination lock dance next to someone who is giving you the silent treatment is the worst. 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, about my face. Yeah, everyone says they don't really see it, but I definitely do. It's just so obvious to me.

Um, I'm not really sure if I'd say it affects my entire worth. Like, I don't know if it's the absolute center of my life, it's just... on my mind. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah, I definitely do that stuff. Like, before I send out any event pictures to clients or if I have to hop on a Zoom call with a vendor, I'm always checking my face in my phone camera. I'll take a bunch of selfies, retake them, try to tilt my head just right so it doesn't look so uneven, or I'll ask my coworker if I look weird. It actually eats up so much time that I end up falling behind on my planning tasks. 

Which is so frustrating because I really need to stay on top of my schedule this week. We have this huge tech convention coming up, and they want these interactive badges for all the attendees. They're supposed to light up when you're near someone with similar interests, which sounds cool in theory, but the software is so glitchy. My cousin tried to develop an app like that once. It was for finding people who wanted to play pick-up basketball at local parks. He spent, like, six months coding it in his basement, but he forgot to include a feature to filter by location, so people in New York were getting matched with people in California. He was so mad when he realized. 

He's always been kind of hot-headed, though. When we were kids, if he lost at Monopoly, he would literally flip the board over. Those little silver pieces would go flying everywhere. I stepped on the thimble once and it hurt so bad. It's worse than stepping on a Lego, honestly. Legos are everywhere in my house right now because my nephew is staying with me for the week, and he is obsessed with building these giant pirate ships. 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, about checking mirrors and stuff. Yeah, I do that a lot. Before meetings and pictures, mostly. It takes up a lot of time.

I just haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing certain things over and over again—like checking mirrors, taking selfies, comparing your face to others, or trying to cover it up—because of how you feel about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I definitely do that stuff. Like, before I send out any event pictures to clients or if I have to hop on a Zoom call with a vendor, I'm always checking my face in my phone camera. It actually eats up so much time that I end up falling behind on my planning tasks. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I really need to stay on top of my schedule this week. We have this huge tech convention coming up, and they want these interactive badges for all the attendees. They're supposed to light up when you're near someone with similar interests, which sounds cool in theory, but the software is so glitchy. My cousin tried to develop an app like that once. It was for finding people who wanted to play pick-up basketball at local parks. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, I haven't really turned down a job or anything like that, but... it definitely messes with my work. Like, I actually missed part of a planning deadline recently because I spent so much time obsessing over how my face looked in the event materials. And now, getting ready for vendor calls and meeting prep just takes me way longer than it should because I'm so focused on it. 

Which is honestly the worst timing, because those vendor calls are already so stressful. Last week, I was on the phone with this caterer for like an hour because they tried to switch out the mini beef Wellingtons for these weird mushroom tartlets without telling us. And you can't just do that. People expect the Wellingtons. My uncle is a caterer, actually, and he always says that appetizers make or break an event. He catered my cousin's wedding last year, and he made this incredible bacon-wrapped scallop thing. But the wedding itself was kind of a disaster because the DJ played the wrong song for the first dance. They wanted this super romantic acoustic cover, and he played the original upbeat pop version. The bride was trying to slow dance to it, and it just looked so awkward. 

I felt so bad for her, but she's always been kind of high-strung anyway. She used to make us practice our bridesmaid walk down the hallway of her apartment, like, ten times a day. And her cat kept attacking the train of my dress. It was this gorgeous chiffon material, too. I had to take it to a special dry cleaner just to get the claw marks buffed out, and the guy there was so rude about it. He acted like I was the one who dragged it through a briar patch or something. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, about work and stuff. Yeah, it definitely slows me down with my deadlines and prep time. It's just really frustrating.

The developer promised it would sync offline, but it totally didn't. So when the Wi-Fi went down—because of course it did, hotel Wi-Fi is a scam, I swear they throttle it on purpose just so you have to pay for the premium tier—none of the VIP badges printed. And this one guy, he was like, some VP of marketing for a tech startup, he got so mad at me. He was wearing this really loud plaid suit, which honestly was a choice, but he just stood there tapping his foot while I tried to reset the iPad. I hate iPads for events. The screens get so smudgy, and people always press way too hard on them. \n\nBut, um, yeah. I guess I don't completely avoid things, I just try to hide as much as I can while still doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concerns about the unevenness of your face cause you more distress or difficulty functioning than most people would experience in similar situations?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, definitely more than normal people. Like, I know I've brought this up a couple times already, but it's just really getting to me that I missed that big planning deadline just because I was obsessing over how I looked in the event materials. Normal people don't do that. And the fact that basic meeting prep and vendor calls are taking me way longer than they should now... it just makes me feel like I'm falling behind. \n\nWhich is so stressful because I have to be on the phone with these vendors constantly. Actually, I'm dreading calling our catering vendor back this afternoon. They just switched to this new seasonal menu and it is so confusing. They don't label any of the allergens clearly. Like, they have this wrap called the \"Autumn Harvest\" and it doesn't even say if there are nuts in it. You have to be so careful with peanut allergies now. We had an event last year where a guest had an allergic reaction because one of the servers used the same tongs for the pecan salad and the regular house salad, and it was terrifying. The ambulance had to come, and the sirens completely interrupted the keynote speaker. \n\nHe was this guy who wrote a book about, like, corporate synergy or something, but honestly he was incredibly boring anyway. He just kept pacing back and forth across the stage, and I noticed his shoes were super scuffed. Like, if you're getting paid five thousand dollars to speak, you can at least polish your loafers, you know? \n\nUm... wait. Sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, right, if it causes me more distress. Yeah. I definitely think it's worse for me than for most people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unevenness on your face is something that most people would not even notice, or do you believe it's very obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I think it's incredibly obvious. Like, that's actually why I came in to see you in the first place. Ever since I got promoted and I have to be more in front of people at work, I just... I can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in photos. It's all I see. But when I point it out to my friends or my coworkers, they always say they barely see anything. Which is just crazy to me, because it's right there. \n\nBut people are so unobservant anyway, you know? Like, my roommate, she literally didn't notice that our apartment building repainted the entire lobby. It used to be this really awful, depressing beige color, and they painted it this light sage green over the weekend. It smells like fresh paint everywhere. We went to high school together, and she used to date this guy who was so obviously cheating on her with a girl from the cheerleading squad. Everyone knew. He would literally leave his phone unlocked on the cafeteria table and you could see the texts popping up. But she just thought he was \"really popular.\" I tried to tell her once, but she got so mad at me, so I just dropped it. We didn't talk for like three weeks, which was super awkward because we shared a locker. Having to do that little combination lock dance next to someone who is giving you the silent treatment is the worst. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, about my face. Yeah, everyone says they don't really see it, but I definitely do. It's just so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your face has become a central focus in your life, such that it's almost always on your mind and affecting how you see yourself and your worth?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure if I'd say it affects my entire worth. Like, I don't know if it's the absolute center of my life, it's just... on my mind. But central focus... honestly, speaking of central focus, all I can think about right now are the centerpieces for this charity gala I'm working on next month. It is a complete nightmare. The florist completely messed up the sample arrangement yesterday. We specifically asked for dusty blue hydrangeas, and she brought these bright, almost neon royal blue ones. They completely clashed with the table linens, which are this really expensive champagne silk. \n\nAnd silk is so hard to work with anyway because it stains if you even look at it wrong. I actually bought what I thought was a silk blouse online for work last month? Total scam. The website looked super legit, but it shipped from overseas and took six weeks, and when it finally got here, it was one hundred percent polyester. The static cling was out of control. I tried that trick where you rub a dryer sheet on the inside of it, but it just made me smell like aggressively strong lavender all day. I had a headache by noon. I can't stand artificial lavender, it always smells like cheap bathroom cleaner, you know? Um, yeah, I mean, I think I just mentioned this, but it definitely causes problems at work. Like, I completely missed part of a planning deadline recently because I was just... I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. And now, just getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings takes me way longer than it should.

I tried to clean them with that special suede eraser thing, but it just made a weird bald spot on the toe. 

I was so upset because I got them on sale at this boutique downtown that's closing. It's so sad how many small businesses are shutting down right now. There was this amazing little bakery next to my apartment that made the best almond croissants, and they just closed last week. The owner said the rent got too high. Now I have to go to the chain coffee shop on the corner, and their pastries always taste like they've been sitting in a plastic wrapper for three days. You know that weird, artificial vanilla taste? I can't stand it. It reminds me of those cheap car air fresheners. My dad used to hang like three of those pine tree ones in his truck, and it was so overpowering. 

But, um, wait, what were we talking about? Oh, if it causes me distress or problems. Yeah, like I said, it definitely messes with my deadlines and makes meeting prep take forever. It's just really hard to function normally when I'm so focused on it.

Even when everyone else tells me they barely see anything, which I don't understand how they couldn't. \n\nBut yeah, the events... like last weekend, we were running this huge corporate retreat downtown, and the floral arrangements were completely wrong. It was a total nightmare, and the caterer was late on top of it, so my boss was freaking out and making me call everyone... but anyway, wait, what was I saying? \n\nOh, right. The photos from those events. I just can't stop looking at how uneven that part of my face is whenever they post the recaps online.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or self-conscious when you're in front of people, especially because of how your face looks in photos or in person?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I get so anxious. Like, extremely self-conscious when I have to stand at the front and greet everyone, because I just know they're staring right at my face. Which is already stressful enough, but then at that same retreat last weekend, our entire check-in app crashed right when the biggest rush of people arrived. \n\nWe had these rented iPads, right? And the venue promised us this dedicated Wi-Fi network so this exact thing wouldn't happen, but it kept dropping out every five minutes. So I had a line of like fifty corporate executives just standing there getting mad while I was trying to manually dig out their name badges from these huge plastic bins. And the hotel's IT guy took literally half an hour to come help us, and when he finally showed up, he didn't even know the admin password for the router! It was just... um, wait, sorry. Did that answer your question? I just get really nervous, yeah.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the unevenness of that part of your face, and does it interfere with your work or daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, how much time? God, it feels like all the time. Like, hours. I actually missed part of a big planning deadline recently because I spent so much time just... staring at how I looked in the draft event materials. I was supposed to be approving the proofs for these glossy brochures, but I couldn't get past my face in the staff photo section. \n\nWhich reminds me, the printing company we use has been so difficult lately. We switched to them because they promised eco-friendly ink, but the colors always come out looking completely washed out. Like, the navy blue logo on our last batch looked basically gray, and my boss was furious. I had to spend three hours on the phone arguing with their account manager, who kept insisting it was an issue with our PDF file, which is ridiculous because our graphic designer checked it twice. And then they tried to charge us a rush fee to reprint them! The nerve of some of these vendors, honestly. \n\nBut yeah, um, it definitely interferes. Vendor calls and meeting prep take me way longer than they should now because I'm just so distracted by it.\n\nDoctor: Does the way your face looks in photos ever make it hard for you to do your job, interact with others, or enjoy activities you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, like I was just saying a second ago, it definitely makes my job hard. I actually missed that planning deadline because I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in those event materials. And now, just doing basic stuff... like vendor calls and meeting prep, they take me way longer than they should because I'm so distracted by it. \n\nEspecially if it's a video call. Zoom is honestly the worst thing ever invented. They updated the interface like a month ago, and now the self-view window is stuck in this weird spot unless you manually pin it. My boss is completely technologically illiterate, so he spent twenty minutes of our all-hands meeting yesterday just trying to figure out how to mute himself. We could hear his dog barking the entire time. It's a Golden Retriever, I think? Or maybe a Lab mix, I don't know, it's super cute but so loud. He brings it into the office sometimes on Fridays and it sheds everywhere, which is an absolute nightmare because I always wear black slacks, so I have to keep a lint roller at my desk... \n\nUm, sorry. I completely lost my train of thought. What were we talking about? Did I answer the thing about my job?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself avoiding meetings, events, or interactions with others because you’re worried about how your face looks in photos or in person?\n\nPatient: Um, avoiding things? I mean... maybe? I don't know if I fully avoid them, but I definitely dread them. Like, I'll still go, but I'll try to stand in the back or volunteer to run the registration table so I don't have to mingle. \n\nActually, speaking of registration tables, we used this new app for check-ins last week and it was a total disaster. The developer promised it would sync offline, but it totally didn't. So when the Wi-Fi went down—because of course it did, hotel Wi-Fi is a scam, I swear they throttle it on purpose just so you have to pay for the premium tier—none of the VIP badges printed. And this one guy, he was like, some VP of marketing for a tech startup, he got so mad at me. He was wearing this really loud plaid suit, which honestly was a choice, but he just stood there tapping his foot while I tried to reset the iPad. I hate iPads for events. The screens get so smudgy, and people always press way too hard on them. \n\nBut, um, yeah. I guess I don't completely avoid things, I just try to hide as much as I can while still doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concerns about the unevenness of your face cause you more distress or difficulty functioning than most people would experience in similar situations?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, definitely more than normal people. Like, I know I've brought this up a couple times already, but it's just really getting to me that I missed that big planning deadline just because I was obsessing over how I looked in the event materials. Normal people don't do that. And the fact that basic meeting prep and vendor calls are taking me way longer than they should now... it just makes me feel like I'm falling behind. \n\nWhich is so stressful because I have to be on the phone with these vendors constantly. Actually, I'm dreading calling our catering vendor back this afternoon. They just switched to this new seasonal menu and it is so confusing. They don't label any of the allergens clearly. Like, they have this wrap called the \"Autumn Harvest\" and it doesn't even say if there are nuts in it. You have to be so careful with peanut allergies now. We had an event last year where a guest had an allergic reaction because one of the servers used the same tongs for the pecan salad and the regular house salad, and it was terrifying. The ambulance had to come, and the sirens completely interrupted the keynote speaker. \n\nHe was this guy who wrote a book about, like, corporate synergy or something, but honestly he was incredibly boring anyway. He just kept pacing back and forth across the stage, and I noticed his shoes were super scuffed. Like, if you're getting paid five thousand dollars to speak, you can at least polish your loafers, you know? \n\nUm... wait. Sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, right, if it causes me more distress. Yeah. I definitely think it's worse for me than for most people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unevenness on your face is something that most people would not even notice, or do you believe it's very obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I think it's incredibly obvious. Like, that's actually why I came in to see you in the first place. Ever since I got promoted and I have to be more in front of people at work, I just... I can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in photos. It's all I see. But when I point it out to my friends or my coworkers, they always say they barely see anything. Which is just crazy to me, because it's right there. \n\nBut people are so unobservant anyway, you know? Like, my roommate, she literally didn't notice that our apartment building repainted the entire lobby. It used to be this really awful, depressing beige color, and they painted it this light sage green over the weekend. It smells like fresh paint everywhere. And she walked in on Monday and was like, \"Did they change the lightbulbs in here?\" Like, how do you not notice a completely different color on the walls? \n\nShe's always been kind of oblivious, though. We went to high school together, and she used to date this guy who was so obviously cheating on her with a girl from the cheerleading squad. Everyone knew. He would literally leave his phone unlocked on the cafeteria table and you could see the texts popping up. But she just thought he was \"really popular.\" I tried to tell her once, but she got so mad at me, so I just dropped it. We didn't talk for like three weeks, which was super awkward because we shared a locker. Having to do that little combination lock dance next to someone who is giving you the silent treatment is the worst. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, about my face. Yeah, everyone says they don't really see it, but I definitely do. It's just so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your face has become a central focus in your life, such that it's almost always on your mind and affecting how you see yourself and your worth?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure if I'd say it affects my entire worth. Like, I don't know if it's the absolute center of my life, it's just... on my mind. But central focus... honestly, speaking of central focus, all I can think about right now are the centerpieces for this charity gala I'm working on next month. It is a complete nightmare. The florist completely messed up the sample arrangement yesterday. We specifically asked for dusty blue hydrangeas, and she brought these bright, almost neon royal blue ones. They completely clashed with the table linens, which are this really expensive champagne silk. \n\nAnd silk is so hard to work with anyway because it stains if you even look at it wrong. I actually bought what I thought was a silk blouse online for work last month? Total scam. The website looked super legit, but it shipped from overseas and took six weeks, and when it finally got here, it was one hundred percent polyester. The static cling was out of control. I tried that trick where you rub a dryer sheet on the inside of it, but it just made me smell like aggressively strong lavender all day. I had a headache by noon. I can't stand artificial lavender, it always smells like cheap bathroom cleaner, you know? My grandma used to use this lavender spray in her guest bathroom and it always made me sneeze.\n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask me? If it's my central focus? Yeah, I mean, I don't really know if it defines my whole worth or anything like that. I just haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing certain things over and over again—like checking mirrors, taking selfies, comparing your face to others, or trying to cover it up—because of how you feel about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I definitely do that stuff. Like, before I send out any event pictures to clients or if I have to hop on a Zoom call with a vendor, I'm always checking my face in my phone camera. I'll take a bunch of selfies, retake them, try to tilt my head just right so it doesn't look so uneven, or I'll ask my coworker if I look weird. It actually eats up so much time that I end up falling behind on my planning tasks. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I really need to stay on top of my schedule this week. We have this huge tech convention coming up, and they want these interactive badges for all the attendees. They're supposed to light up when you're near someone with similar interests, which sounds cool in theory, but the software is so glitchy. My cousin tried to develop an app like that once. It was for finding people who wanted to play pick-up basketball at local parks. He spent, like, six months coding it in his basement, but he forgot to include a feature to filter by location, so people in New York were getting matched with people in California. He was so mad when he realized. \n\nHe's always been kind of hot-headed, though. When we were kids, if he lost at Monopoly, he would literally flip the board over. Those little silver pieces would go flying everywhere. I stepped on the thimble once and it hurt so bad. It's worse than stepping on a Lego, honestly. Legos are everywhere in my house right now because my nephew is staying with me for the week, and he is obsessed with building these giant pirate ships. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, about checking mirrors and stuff. Yeah, I do that a lot. Before meetings and pictures, mostly. It takes up a lot of time.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your face ever caused you to turn down a job opportunity, avoid social events outside of work, or lose interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I haven't really turned down a job or anything like that, but... it definitely messes with my work. Like, I actually missed part of a planning deadline recently because I spent so much time obsessing over how my face looked in the event materials. And now, getting ready for vendor calls and meeting prep just takes me way longer than it should because I'm so focused on it. \n\nWhich is honestly the worst timing, because those vendor calls are already so stressful. Last week, I was on the phone with this caterer for like an hour because they tried to switch out the mini beef Wellingtons for these weird mushroom tartlets without telling us. And you can't just do that. People expect the Wellingtons. My uncle is a caterer, actually, and he always says that appetizers make or break an event. He catered my cousin's wedding last year, and he made this incredible bacon-wrapped scallop thing. But the wedding itself was kind of a disaster because the DJ played the wrong song for the first dance. They wanted this super romantic acoustic cover, and he played the original upbeat pop version. The bride was trying to slow dance to it, and it just looked so awkward. \n\nI felt so bad for her, but she's always been kind of high-strung anyway. She used to make us practice our bridesmaid walk down the hallway of her apartment, like, ten times a day. And her cat kept attacking the train of my dress. It was this gorgeous chiffon material, too. I had to take it to a special dry cleaner just to get the claw marks buffed out, and the guy there was so rude about it. He acted like I was the one who dragged it through a briar patch or something. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, about work and stuff. Yeah, it definitely slows me down with my deadlines and prep time. It's just really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concerns about your face have caused you significant distress or difficulties functioning in areas like work, social situations, or other important parts of your life, beyond what most people would normally experience?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean, I think I just mentioned this, but it definitely causes problems at work. Like, I completely missed part of a planning deadline recently because I was just... I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. And now, just getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings takes me way longer than it should. \n\nWhich is so stressful because my boss is a stickler for punctuality. He actually fired a guy last month for being ten minutes late to a client walkthrough. Though, to be fair, that guy was always late. He blamed it on the train schedule, but I take the same train line and it's almost never delayed. Well, except for that one time last winter when it snowed like three inches and the entire city just shut down. I was stuck on the platform for two hours freezing my toes off. I was wearing these really cute suede boots, but they were definitely not waterproof. The salt on the sidewalks completely ruined them. I tried to clean them with that special suede eraser thing, but it just made a weird bald spot on the toe. \n\nI was so upset because I got them on sale at this boutique downtown that's closing. It's so sad how many small businesses are shutting down right now. There was this amazing little bakery next to my apartment that made the best almond croissants, and they just closed last week. The owner said the rent got too high. Now I have to go to the chain coffee shop on the corner, and their pastries always taste like they've been sitting in a plastic wrapper for three days. You know that weird, artificial vanilla taste? I can't stand it. It reminds me of those cheap car air fresheners. My dad used to hang like three of those pine tree ones in his truck, and it was so overpowering. \n\nBut, um, wait, what were we talking about? Oh, if it causes me distress or problems. Yeah, like I said, it definitely messes with my deadlines and makes meeting prep take forever. It's just really hard to function normally when I'm so focused on it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, yeah, I mean, I guess it does cause distress, especially with work. Like, I missed part of a planning deadline recently because I just spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. My friend has a dog like that—a pug—and it just breathes so loudly.

The florist completely messed up the sample arrangement yesterday. We specifically asked for dusty blue hydrangeas, and she brought these bright, almost neon royal blue ones. They completely clashed with the table linens, which are this really expensive champagne silk. \n\nAnd silk is so hard to work with anyway because it stains if you even look at it wrong. I actually bought what I thought was a silk blouse online for work last month? Total scam. The website looked super legit, but it shipped from overseas and took six weeks, and when it finally got here, it was one hundred percent polyester. The static cling was out of control. I tried that trick where you rub a dryer sheet on the inside of it, but it just made me smell like aggressively strong lavender all day. I had a headache by noon. I can't stand artificial lavender, it always smells like cheap bathroom cleaner, you know? My grandma used to use this lavender spray in her guest bathroom and it always made me sneeze.\n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask me? If it's my central focus? Yeah, I mean, I don't really know if it defines my whole worth or anything like that. I just haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing certain things over and over again—like checking mirrors, taking selfies, comparing your face to others, or trying to cover it up—because of how you feel about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I definitely do that stuff. Like, before I send out any event pictures to clients or if I have to hop on a Zoom call with a vendor, I'm always checking my face in my phone camera. I'll take a bunch of selfies, retake them, try to tilt my head just right so it doesn't look so uneven, or I'll ask my coworker if I look weird. It actually eats up so much time that I end up falling behind on my planning tasks. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I really need to stay on top of my schedule this week. We have this huge tech convention coming up, and they want these interactive badges for all the attendees. They're supposed to light up when you're near someone with similar interests, which sounds cool in theory, but the software is so glitchy. My cousin tried to develop an app like that once. It was for finding people who wanted to play pick-up basketball at local parks. He spent, like, six months coding it in his basement, but he forgot to include a feature to filter by location, so people in New York were getting matched with people in California. He was so mad when he realized. \n\nHe's always been kind of hot-headed, though. When we were kids, if he lost at Monopoly, he would literally flip the board over. Those little silver pieces would go flying everywhere. I stepped on the thimble once and it hurt so bad. It's worse than stepping on a Lego, honestly. Legos are everywhere in my house right now because my nephew is staying with me for the week, and he is obsessed with building these giant pirate ships. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, about checking mirrors and stuff. Yeah, I do that a lot. Before meetings and pictures, mostly. It takes up a lot of time.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your face ever caused you to turn down a job opportunity, avoid social events outside of work, or lose interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I haven't really turned down a job or anything like that, but... it definitely messes with my work. Like, I actually missed part of a planning deadline recently because I spent so much time obsessing over how my face looked in the event materials. And now, getting ready for vendor calls and meeting prep just takes me way longer than it should because I'm so focused on it. \n\nWhich is honestly the worst timing, because those vendor calls are already so stressful. Last week, I was on the phone with this caterer for like an hour because they tried to switch out the mini beef Wellingtons for these weird mushroom tartlets without telling us. And you can't just do that. People expect the Wellingtons. My uncle is a caterer, actually, and he always says that appetizers make or break an event. He catered my cousin's wedding last year, and he made this incredible bacon-wrapped scallop thing. But the wedding itself was kind of a disaster because the DJ played the wrong song for the first dance. They wanted this super romantic acoustic cover, and he played the original upbeat pop version. The bride was trying to slow dance to it, and it just looked so awkward. \n\nI felt so bad for her, but she's always been kind of high-strung anyway. She used to make us practice our bridesmaid walk down the hallway of her apartment, like, ten times a day. And her cat kept attacking the train of my dress. It was this gorgeous chiffon material, too. I had to take it to a special dry cleaner just to get the claw marks buffed out, and the guy there was so rude about it. He acted like I was the one who dragged it through a briar patch or something. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, about work and stuff. Yeah, it definitely slows me down with my deadlines and prep time. It's just really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concerns about your face have caused you significant distress or difficulties functioning in areas like work, social situations, or other important parts of your life, beyond what most people would normally experience?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean, I think I just mentioned this, but it definitely causes problems at work. Like, I completely missed part of a planning deadline recently because I was just... I spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. And now, just getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings takes me way longer than it should. \n\nWhich is so stressful because my boss is a stickler for punctuality. He actually fired a guy last month for being ten minutes late to a client walkthrough. Though, to be fair, that guy was always late. He blamed it on the train schedule, but I take the same train line and it's almost never delayed. Well, except for that one time last winter when it snowed like three inches and the entire city just shut down. I was stuck on the platform for two hours freezing my toes off. I was wearing these really cute suede boots, but they were definitely not waterproof. The salt on the sidewalks completely ruined them. I tried to clean them with that special suede eraser thing, but it just made a weird bald spot on the toe. \n\nI was so upset because I got them on sale at this boutique downtown that's closing. It's so sad how many small businesses are shutting down right now. There was this amazing little bakery next to my apartment that made the best almond croissants, and they just closed last week. The owner said the rent got too high. Now I have to go to the chain coffee shop on the corner, and their pastries always taste like they've been sitting in a plastic wrapper for three days. You know that weird, artificial vanilla taste? I can't stand it. It reminds me of those cheap car air fresheners. My dad used to hang like three of those pine tree ones in his truck, and it was so overpowering. \n\nBut, um, wait, what were we talking about? Oh, if it causes me distress or problems. Yeah, like I said, it definitely messes with my deadlines and makes meeting prep take forever. It's just really hard to function normally when I'm so focused on it.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about how your face looks ever caused you to significantly reduce your participation in work, social events, or other important activities?\n\nPatient: Um, I feel like a broken record here, but yeah, like I was just saying, it definitely cuts into my work. I don't really drop out of things completely, but I did miss that planning deadline because I was so caught up in how my face looked in the event materials, and I just couldn't stop checking it. And because of that, getting ready for vendor calls and doing my meeting prep takes me way longer than it's supposed to. \n\nWhich was a huge headache with those event materials, actually, because the print shop guy I use, Gary, is super strict about his 3 PM cutoff. Meatball snores so loudly you can barely hear Gary talk over the phone. I really want a dog, actually, maybe a golden retriever, but my landlord has this ridiculous rule about no pets over twenty pounds. It makes zero sense because small dogs are the destructive ones! My friend has this tiny Jack Russell terrier that literally chewed a hole straight through the drywall in her apartment. Just ate the wall. She had to pay so much money to fix it when her lease was up. Like, I still go to work, obviously, and I still try to go to my spin class on Thursdays. Though, actually, I did skip spin class last week, but that wasn't because of my face. It was because the instructor, Kyle, totally changed his playlist without warning. He usually does this really fun 2000s throwback ride, like Britney and NSYNC, but he switched to this hardcore EDM stuff. The bass was so loud it literally made my teeth rattle. \n\nI hate that feeling. My dentist actually told me I need a night guard because I grind my teeth in my sleep, probably from stress. But those custom guards are like five hundred dollars! My dental insurance doesn't cover it because they consider it \"preventative\" or whatever. Insurance is such a scam. I tried to call them to argue about a co-pay once, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to the worst elevator music. It sounded like a pan flute cover of that Celine Dion song from Titanic. I love Celine Dion, though. My mom and I went to see her in Vegas for my eighteenth birthday, and it was incredible. She wore this sparkly silver gown that caught the light perfectly, and the stage design was just... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you just ask me? Did I stop doing things? Oh, right. No, no, I haven't stopped doing anything completely. It's just... yeah, I still do my stuff, I just obsess over it and it takes me a lot longer, like I was saying before. But I haven't just quit anything entirely.\n\nDoctor: Beyond work, do you feel that your concern about your face has affected your personal relationships, your self-esteem, or your ability to enjoy life the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I'm not really sure if it's affected my relationships or anything like that. I mean, I still hang out with my friends and stuff. We usually go to this trivia night on Tuesdays at a pub near my place. It's actually really fun, though the questions have gotten way too hard lately. Last week they asked about some obscure 19th-century Russian author, and literally no one knew the answer except this one guy in the corner who looked like he hadn't slept in three days. Like, I'm not paying two hundred dollars for a jacket that smells like mothballs. \n\nMy grandma's attic smells exactly like mothballs. We used to play up there when I was a kid, even though we weren't supposed to because the floorboards were super weak. My cousin actually stepped right through the ceiling once. His leg was just dangling over the dining room table right in the middle of Thanksgiving dinner! My aunt screamed so loud she dropped the gravy boat, and it shattered everywhere. Like, I still go to work, obviously, and I still try to go to my spin class on Thursdays. Though, actually, I did skip spin class last week, but that wasn't because of my face. It was because the instructor, Kyle, totally changed his playlist without warning. He usually does this really fun 2000s throwback ride, like Britney and NSYNC, but he switched to this hardcore EDM stuff. The bass was so loud it literally made my teeth rattle. \n\nI hate that feeling. My dentist actually told me I need a night guard because I grind my teeth in my sleep, probably from stress. But those custom guards are like five hundred dollars! My dental insurance doesn't cover it because they consider it \"preventative\" or whatever. Insurance is such a scam. I tried to call them to argue about a co-pay once, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to the worst elevator music. It sounded like a pan flute cover of that Celine Dion song from Titanic. I love Celine Dion, though. My mom and I went to see her in Vegas for my eighteenth birthday, and it was incredible. She wore this sparkly silver gown that caught the light perfectly, and the stage design was just... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you just ask me? Did I stop doing things? Oh, right. No, no, I haven't stopped doing anything completely. It's just... yeah, I still do my stuff, I just obsess over it and it takes me a lot longer, like I was saying before. But I haven't just quit anything entirely.\n\nDoctor: Beyond work, do you feel that your concern about your face has affected your personal relationships, your self-esteem, or your ability to enjoy life the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I'm not really sure if it's affected my relationships or anything like that. I mean, I still hang out with my friends and stuff. We usually go to this trivia night on Tuesdays at a pub near my place. It's actually really fun, though the questions have gotten way too hard lately. Last week they asked about some obscure 19th-century Russian author, and literally no one knew the answer except this one guy in the corner who looked like he hadn't slept in three days. He was wearing this really cool vintage leather jacket, though. I've been looking for one just like it on Depop, but everyone prices them so ridiculously high. Like, I'm not paying two hundred dollars for a jacket that smells like mothballs. \n\nMy grandma's attic smells exactly like mothballs. We used to play up there when I was a kid, even though we weren't supposed to because the floorboards were super weak. My cousin actually stepped right through the ceiling once. His leg was just dangling over the dining room table right in the middle of Thanksgiving dinner! My aunt screamed so loud she dropped the gravy boat, and it shattered everywhere. It was this beautiful antique china set she got for her wedding, and you literally can't find replacements for it anywhere now. She tried looking on eBay for like six months but finally gave up. \n\nWait, what were you asking about? Oh, my self-esteem and relationships? Yeah, I don't know, I haven't really noticed a huge change there. I still do my normal stuff with my friends, I just... I don't know, I guess I just haven't really thought about it that much outside of how it messes with my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform certain routines or behaviors—like checking your appearance, adjusting how you look, or seeking reassurance—because it helps relieve the anxiety you feel about your face?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I definitely do that. Like I was saying earlier about work, before I send out any event pictures or join a vendor meeting, I'll just sit there checking my face in my phone camera over and over. I'll take a million photos, trying to tilt my head just the right way so I don't look awful, or I'll ask my coworker if I look weird. It just eats up so much time that I fall behind on my actual planning stuff. \n\nWhich is so stressful because event planning is all about timing. Like, if you don't book the catering early enough, you end up with the worst options. We had this one event where the only caterer left was this place that specialized in artisanal hot dogs. Which, okay, sounds fun, but they put the weirdest toppings on them. Like, who wants a hot dog with crushed pineapple and spicy mayo? It was a disaster. Half the guests wouldn't eat them, and this one lady actually complained to my boss that the hot dog ruined her silk blouse because the mayo dripped everywhere. Silk is so impossible to clean, though. I bought a silk pillowcase once because I read on TikTok that it's supposed to be better for your skin and hair, and it was so expensive. But then I accidentally threw it in the regular wash with my towels, and it completely shrank. It literally looks like it belongs on a doll's bed now. My niece actually has this huge American Girl doll collection, and she...\n\nWait, sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, routines and checking. Yeah, I just... I check my phone camera a lot and ask my coworkers if I look okay, and it just makes me run late on everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your concern about your face causes you significant distress or makes it hard for you to function normally in your daily life, even outside of work situations?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean, I guess it does cause distress, especially with work. Like, I missed part of a planning deadline recently because I just spent so much time dealing with how I looked in the event materials. And now, like, getting ready for vendor calls and meeting prep takes me way longer than it should because I'm just so stuck on it. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I used to be so fast at prepping. I had this whole system with color-coded sticky notes. I love office supplies. I went to this stationary store last weekend that had these imported Japanese pens, and they write so smoothly. It’s like butter. But they were, like, eight dollars a pen! Who pays eight dollars for a pen? My brother actually collects vintage fountain pens, which I think is kind of weird, but he spends hundreds of dollars on them. He keeps them in this special velvet-lined display case in his living room. His cat, Mr. Whiskers, knocked the case over once, and I thought my brother was going to cry. Mr. Whiskers is a menace, honestly. He’s an orange tabby, and I swear he has exactly one brain cell. He just stares at the wall for hours. My friend has a dog like that—a pug—and it just breathes so loudly. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, distress and functioning. Yeah, it just... it makes my work prep take forever, and missing that deadline was really bad. But outside of work, I don't know, I guess I just mostly notice it when I'm trying to get my job done.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel too self-conscious about your face to enjoy time with friends or try new things, like going out to dinner or posting photos on social media?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I said, I still go to trivia on Tuesdays and stuff. Like, last week I got targeted for this weird LED face mask that's supposed to make you look ten years younger, but it honestly looked like something out of a horror movie. My friend actually bought one, and she scared her boyfriend so badly when he came home early from work. Getting grease out of a carpet is literally impossible. We had to rent one of those industrial steam cleaners when I moved out of my last apartment because my roommate spilled an entire bottle of olive oil in the kitchen. She was trying to make this viral focaccia bread recipe, but she forgot to put the lid back on the bottle. The bread didn't even turn out good. It was super dense, like a brick. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, friends and social media. Yeah, I don't know, I just don't post much anyway, and I still go to trivia, so I guess I don't really feel like it stops me from doing normal stuff outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you feel that your concern about your face ever causes you significant distress or makes it hard for you to function normally in your personal life or social situations?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really think so.
